# AI Media Scout — 2026-10-17

Today’s expanded review adds perceptual art and engraving/descreening to the effective search plan. Full guides cover transformed-view diffusion, interactive visual studios, game music, neural video preparation and earlier screened creative production tools. Every included software license was read in full or compared against a complete previously reviewed grant; model, asset and service limits remain separate. These are documentation reviews, not installations or hands-on output tests. Quality unverified remains the default when evidence is incomplete; Experimental is reserved for explicit current implementation limitations. Existing Upscayl and Nerfstudio evidence notes also receive targeted support follow-ups: user-reported workarounds are recorded, while remaining workflow gaps keep both Quality unverified. The library gains 41 distinct tools; 18 earlier screened entries become full guides. The complete retained catalog was scanned at the metadata level, followed by bounded manual metadata review and targeted primary-source reads beyond the automatic README shortlist, including a final pass after collection. This was not a manual eligibility review of every search result; raw unreviewed matches remain private. All 434 planned repository queries and 17 model tasks were attempted across 58 fields. Repository search outcomes: 434 successful, 0 partial and 0 failed; 108 reached result bounds. Model query failures: 0. Coverage is not exhaustive. All eight configured web ecosystems have researched findings. Some primary pages returned script shells or failed to load; these limitations are disclosed in the field and source-group notes. Model metadata is a discovery signal, not permission to publish unreviewed software. The collection records 114 warnings, including source retrieval and bounded-search disclosures retained in the report coverage details.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Read style, restoration and transformed-view diffusion primary pages. Completed guides distinguish ordinary image processing, learned inference and agent-authored graphics, with weight restrictions visible. [Source 1](https://curryjung.github.io/VisualStylePrompt/) [Source 2](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) |
| Video, animation & film | Reviewed trajectory-controlled animation, local film production and neural interpolation. Completed guides separate preview images, generated clips, editable timelines and successful final exports. Framebop’s complete local render route is currently confined to an Apple Silicon development build; its packaged renderer and generated moving clips remain unfinished. [Source 1](https://wan-move.github.io/) [Source 2](https://animationai.github.io/AnimateAnything/) [Source 3](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio) |

Added agent-assisted synthesis/live coding and browser semantic music planning; conventional DSP alone does not qualify. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/neutone_sdk/) |

Completed browser Gaussian-photo moves and Blender asset/agent workflows, with proprietary hosts and restricted weights disclosed. A new texture engine’s default development weights were found to be untrained placeholders, so a usable trained upscaler was not inferred. [Source 1](https://materialapprentice.github.io/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |

Added single-image Gaussian camera movement with research-only weight limits and unobserved-region caveats. [Source 1](https://rpg.ifi.uzh.ch/E2VID.html) [Source 2](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) |

| AI landscape, garden and environmental design | Revisited GardenDesigner and screened botanical scene candidates. PlantGen explicitly documents deterministic L-system generation with no AI at runtime, so it was excluded from this AI-only collection. [Source 1](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Neural mosaic and stained-glass media | Read neural-collage representations and completed mosAIc’s agent-authoring guide. Smalti’s conventional optimization and optional GPU acceleration did not establish a learned workflow. [Source 1](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI optical illusions and anamorphic media | New effective field: read Visual Anagrams, Factorized Diffusion and Diffusion Illusions primary pages. Completed two transformed-view guides; the Diffusion Illusions repository lacked a retrieved complete software grant. [Source 1](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/factorized_diffusion/) [Source 3](https://diffusionillusions.com/) |
| AI relief engraving and learned halftoning | New effective field: read primary inverse-halftoning research and full licenses of three implementations. Retained MIT Deep Halftoning as an experimental pending guide; two custom grants were held. No laser process or physical engraving was tested. [Source 1](https://houxianxu.github.io/project/inverse-halftoning) [Source 2](https://github.com/Nikronic/Deep-Halftoning) [Source 3](https://github.com/nicholaslck/PredictiveReversibleHalftoning/blob/main/LICENSE) |

## Search and review counts

58 creative fields; 434/434 repository queries attempted; 17/17 model-task queries attempted. 25211 distinct source candidates and 795 model leads. 48 detailed profiles and 11 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 108 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Read the AI music generator README and root tree through the public API. It documents a Music Transformer MIDI route, but its LICENSE lookup returned 404 and the root tree supplied no complete grant. AudioStellar returned a loading shell. No newly eligible GitLab addition was established in this bounded pass. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/collective.ai%2Fai-music-generator/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=master) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ayrsd/audiostellar/-/blob/master/README.md)
- **codeberg** · searched: Read bounded first-page results for diffusion, neural and generative queries (up to 50 each). Many matches were mirrors, generic ML or conventional generative art; Followed the Raspberry Pi diffusion entry into its actual CPU generation script and complete Apache-2.0 license; it is retained as a screened lead with setup and performance pending. Mitsua weights use separate Open RAIL-M terms, despite public-domain training data. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=50) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=neural&limit=50) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=generative&limit=50)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Read four neural-search results, three diffusion results and the first audio-results page (15 entries, page 1 of 4). Generic ML, conventional audio and existing diffusion clients predominated; no new complete creative AI license review was established. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=neural) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=audio)
- **packages** · searched: Read npm metadata for siljangnim-mcp 0.2.8 (GPL-3.0-or-later, Node 22+) and the SDAI F-Droid listing. PyPI project pages returned challenge text, and an unscoped game-asset-mcp npm lookup returned 404; neither was treated as successful package evidence. [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/siljangnim-mcp/latest) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.shifthackz.aisdv1.app.foss/)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Read the Neutone host and QGIS registry; completed Scenario Blender and agent animation integrations. Required proprietary hosts, hosted models and asset restrictions are separated from the adapter licenses. [Source](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/ai_agent/) [Source](https://www.scenario.com/)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary pages cover every effective field, including transformed-view illusions and neural descreening. Title-only/script-shell pages and the SyncLight certificate failure remain disclosed gaps, not evidence of implemented tools. [Source](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source](https://houxianxu.github.io/project/inverse-halftoning) [Source](https://materialapprentice.github.io/)
- **research-code** · searched: Followed neural collage, optical illusion and halftone research into source and complete software terms. Custom grants, missing licenses and placeholders were held separately from usable reviewed open-source implementations. [Source](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) [Source](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source](https://diffusionillusions.com/)
- **international** · searched: Read Korean/English studio documentation, Russian/English game-music instructions, Chinese presentation/video workflows, Japanese narration documentation and German textile research. Language and star count did not determine quality labels. [Source](https://github.com/okdalto/siljangnim) [Source](https://github.com/V1QR/genmuza) [Source](https://github.com/Cennyk-learner/ppt-cinematic-animation) [Source](https://github.com/takago/NarrationAutomata) [Source](https://www.green-ai-hub.de/pilotprojekte/pilotprojekt-intex-gmbh)

## Visual Anagrams

Images & design · AI optical illusions and anamorphic media · Computational art & creative coding

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-17; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Multi-view optical illusions and reversible illustrations using the documented DeepFloyd IF workflow.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official project page shows rotated, flipped and jigsaw examples; the repository includes animation scripts and a Colab route. [Source](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the documented Linux Conda environment. Obtain access to the gated DeepFloyd checkpoints and authenticate locally before generation. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT software. DeepFloyd IF uses a separate non-commercial research license and gated access; the optional Stable Diffusion upscaler has its own terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Several transforms fail; three or four views are harder. The final upscaler uses only the first prompt and can weaken the illusion. This is not unrestricted commercial image generation.

### How it uses AI

Diffusion sampling combines transformed views so one generated picture changes meaning when rotated, flipped or rearranged. [Source](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

### Introduction

Diffusion sampling combines transformed views so one generated picture changes meaning when rotated, flipped or rearranged. [Source 1](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

### What it is good for

Multi-view optical illusions and reversible illustrations using the documented DeepFloyd IF workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

### Demo & examples

The official project page shows rotated, flipped and jigsaw examples; the repository includes animation scripts and a Colab route. [Source 1](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

### Install

Use the documented Linux Conda environment. Obtain access to the gated DeepFloyd checkpoints and authenticate locally before generation. [Source 1](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented Linux Conda environment. Obtain access to the gated DeepFloyd checkpoints and authenticate locally before generation.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams.git
```


```sh
cd visual_anagrams
```


```sh
conda env create -f environment.yml
```


```sh
conda activate visual_anagrams
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

1. Follow generate.py examples with two prompts and matching views such as identity and rotate_cw.
2. Inspect both interpretations at 64 and 256 pixels before optional 1024-pixel upscaling.
3. Use the documented animate.py workflow to show the transformation.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/dangeng/visual_anagrams/blob/main/readme.md) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/DeepFloyd/IF-I-XL-v1.0)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Conda, Python/PyTorch packages pinned in environment.yml, Hugging Face access and model downloads.
- **Platforms:** The supplied environment is Linux-specific. Colab is another documented route; native Windows and macOS acceptance is not established.

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-17; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: SD3.5 Medium jigsaw and transformed-view image experiments.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/LICENSE)

- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the canonical repository, install its modified diffusers package and the documented Torch/Transformers/Accelerate dependencies, then obtain authorized model access. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT code; SD3.5 Medium is separately governed by Stability AI Community terms. Optional FLUX models have different grants. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Optional FLUX paths are less tested and can perform worse; their large-memory observations are not requirements for the selected SD3.5 route. No physical anamorphic display was tested.

### How it uses AI

A modified diffusion workflow generates two-dimensional illusions with different semantic interpretations under a specified transform. [Source](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

### Introduction

A modified diffusion workflow generates two-dimensional illusions with different semantic interpretations under a specified transform. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

### What it is good for

SD3.5 Medium jigsaw and transformed-view image experiments. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

### Demo & examples

The README supplies paired transformed examples and command-line controls; it distinguishes this work from full 3D Looking Glass anamorphoses. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

### Install

Clone the canonical repository, install its modified diffusers package and the documented Torch/Transformers/Accelerate dependencies, then obtain authorized model access. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the canonical repository, install its modified diffusers package and the documented Torch/Transformers/Accelerate dependencies, then obtain authorized model access.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses.git
```


```sh
cd LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses
```


```sh
pip install -e diffusers/
```


```sh
cd diffusers
```


```sh
python sd3.5.py --prompt1 "a cat" --prompt2 "a puppy" --transform jigsaw --output-dir output
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/LatentGenerativeAnamorphoses/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-3.5-medium)

1. Choose two prompts and one supported transform.
2. Start with SD3.5 Medium and compare both views.
3. Save the settings and inspect transform artifacts before enlarging or printing.

### Introduction

A modular Rust synthesizer exposes its patches and arrangements to AI agents through MCP; the synthesizer DSP itself is conventional. [Source 1](https://github.com/pertyjons/pertylizer/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Agent-assisted patch design, sequencing, offline WAV rendering and OSC-driven performance visuals. [Source 1](https://github.com/pertyjons/pertylizer/blob/main/README.md)

The repository describes included patches, graph editors and the visualizer; these are maintainer examples, not independent output tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/pertyjons/pertylizer/blob/main/README.md)

Build the Rust application, start the editor or headless mode, and connect an MCP-capable agent using the documented local endpoint. [Source 1](https://github.com/pertyjons/pertylizer/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build the Rust application, start the editor or headless mode, and connect an MCP-capable agent using the documented local endpoint.

```sh
cargo build
```


```sh
cargo run
```


```sh
cargo run -- --headless
```


```sh
cargo run -- render --input song.ptz --output song.wav --seconds 60
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/pertyjons/pertylizer/blob/main/README.md)

2. Connect the local MCP endpoint and request a small patch change.
3. Audition the result, save a .ptz arrangement and render a short WAV.

[Source 1](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/rife4_cuda/PROVENANCE.md)

### Install

Set up the documented media gateway/RustFS service and private worker configuration, then build the CUDA worker using the supplied Compose file. [Source 1](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/rife4_cuda/PROVENANCE.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Set up the documented media gateway/RustFS service and private worker configuration, then build the CUDA worker using the supplied Compose file.

```sh
docker compose -f media-worker/docker-compose.yml build
```


```sh
docker compose -f media-worker/docker-compose.yml up -d
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/rife4_cuda/PROVENANCE.md)

1. Check worker health and authenticated gateway connectivity.
2. Upload a short authorized clip and wait for RIFE completion.
3. Review the downloaded interpolation before loading resident frames into the VJ arrangement.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/gordo-v1su4/beatsmaxxer-pro/blob/main/media-worker/rife4_cuda/PROVENANCE.md)

- **Hardware:** The author tested RTX 4070 Ti with 12 GiB VRAM; this is not a declared minimum. Browser and worker memory totals are not documented.
- **Software:** NVIDIA GPU with CUDA, NVDEC/NVENC in Docker; Bun worker, RustFS/media gateway, persistent SQLite work volume and browser WebGPU.
- **Platforms:** CUDA worker deployment is documented; no complete host OS acceptance matrix is provided. There is no CPU interpolation fallback.

The README shows the arrangement interface and describes agent-attributed edits, audio analysis and WAV exports. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

### Install

Build from source while prebuilt releases remain forthcoming. Install stable Rust, Node.js 18+, pnpm and Tauri 2 prerequisites, then start the desktop app before connecting its agent package. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build from source while prebuilt releases remain forthcoming. Install stable Rust, Node.js 18+, pnpm and Tauri 2 prerequisites, then start the desktop app before connecting its agent package.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm tauri dev
```


```sh
npx skills add heshengtao/ai-daw
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Create a small music session and confirm audio output.
2. Connect the MCP-capable agent and ask for a few editable notes or a mix adjustment.
3. Audition, undo unwanted changes and export a short WAV.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Stable Rust/Cargo, Node.js 18+, pnpm and platform Tauri 2 prerequisites. External agent host; optional model downloads and plugins.
- **Platforms:** The project documents Windows x64, Apple Silicon macOS and Linux x64/arm64. Intel Macs are explicitly unsupported in this version.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 app with retained third-party notices, including MPL-2.0 audio decoding. Basic Pitch, LocalVQE, Beat This!, HTDemucs and external agents have separate model/component terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 app with retained third-party notices, including MPL-2.0 audio decoding. Basic Pitch, LocalVQE, Beat This!, HTDemucs and external agents have separate model/component terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

### Limitations

Source build is currently required. CPU inference is documented, but minimum RAM/storage is not. Local audio processing does not establish that the chosen external agent sends no context to its provider. [Source 1](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/heshengtao/ai-daw/blob/main/NOTICE)

### How it uses AI

An iPhone/iPad app optimizes Gaussian splats from ARKit capture using on-device MLX-Swift and differentiable Metal rendering. [Source](https://github.com/shuta-ochiai/Splatelier/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An iPhone/iPad app optimizes Gaussian splats from ARKit capture using on-device MLX-Swift and differentiable Metal rendering. [Source 1](https://github.com/shuta-ochiai/Splatelier/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Capturing small objects/rooms and training viewable Gaussian scenes directly on a mobile Apple device. [Source 1](https://github.com/shuta-ochiai/Splatelier/blob/main/README.md)

2. Build from the official Xcode project on Apple Silicon, select your development team/bundle ID and deploy to a real device with Developer Mode enabled.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/shuta-ochiai/Splatelier.git
```


```sh
cd Splatelier
```


```sh
open App/Splatelier.xcodeproj
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/shuta-ochiai/Splatelier/blob/main/README.md)

2. Stop, choose training iterations/Gaussian cap and monitor heat/throttling.
3. Inspect the model, train further if useful and export PLY or a mesh for separate validation.

[Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Install

Use Python 3.11/3.12 and FFmpeg 6+ with ffprobe. Install the package in a virtual environment, run doctor and separately install an appropriate CUDA PyTorch build if using NVIDIA. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.11/3.12 and FFmpeg 6+ with ffprobe. Install the package in a virtual environment, run doctor and separately install an appropriate CUDA PyTorch build if using NVIDIA.

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
```


```sh
python -m pip install -e .
```


```sh
python -m videocolor doctor
```


```sh
python -m videocolor gui
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Choose a short preview and tiny/large model.
2. Compare colors, motion and scene transitions against the original.
3. Adjust strength/tint if needed, then run the full job and retain the original alongside the AI-colorized copy.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Model downloads are about 220 MB tiny or 912 MB large. The README budgets about 1.7 GiB working space for a 10-second 720p/30fps preview, plus dependencies/models; full jobs can need tens of GiB. RAM/VRAM minimums are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.11/3.12, FFmpeg 6+, ffprobe and Tk for desktop GUI. CPU or NVIDIA CUDA; Apple Metal acceleration is not included.
- **Platforms:** Windows, Linux and Apple Silicon macOS commands are documented; Colab uses external Google infrastructure and quotas.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT app with retained DDColor Apache-2.0 code/model notices. FFmpeg build terms and input-footage rights remain separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT app with retained DDColor Apache-2.0 code/model notices. FFmpeg build terms and input-footage rights remain separate.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Limitations

Early release; colors are inferred, not historically recovered. Flicker and wrong colors remain possible; no face reconstruction, neural upscaling or manual color-guidance feature is included. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/docs/limitations.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukaluka3/videocolor/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Request a short scene and approve its storyboard/timing.
2. Inspect snapshots/contact sheets and revise generated scene/cue code.
3. Render MP4, check frames and audio, and retain the editable project folder.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Headless Chrome adds about 150 MB. The README reports example laptop render times but no minimum RAM/VRAM or total storage.
- **Software:** Claude Code, Node.js 22+, FFmpeg, Python 3.9+ with listed libraries and pinned HyperFrames 0.8.164; optional Piper/system narration.
- **Platforms:** The author reports Windows 11/Git Bash testing and Ubuntu renderer CI. macOS instructions are not independent acceptance evidence.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT package. Bundled GSAP has a separate Standard No Charge license, fonts use SIL OFL, and HyperFrames/Piper/voice models/FFmpeg retain their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT package. Bundled GSAP has a separate Standard No Charge license, fonts use SIL OFL, and HyperFrames/Piper/voice models/FFmpeg retain their own terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Limitations

No photorealism or live facial animation; model-driven authoring produces programmatic graphics rather than neural video frames. Rendering locally does not establish that the external authoring agent sends no context. [Source 1](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/famfamfam/code-cartoon/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

A Blender extension lets an external AI agent inspect a scene, execute Python, render previews and exchange assets through the hosted NOLGIA MCP bridge. ### How it uses AI

The research Piano Studio uses pretrained symbolic-composition and expression transformers locally, then renders the same editable MIDI performance through a recorded piano instrument. Its original sine-reference synth is not a trained RAVE generator. 2. Clone the official source, install Node 22.13+, pnpm 11 and stable Rust/Tauri prerequisites, then run the development app. Select an existing Chrome build for rendering and explicitly approve each model/runtime download. Use the development build for the complete render path.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

1. Create a project, write or draft a short script, then create scenes.
2. Set up approved picture and voice runtimes and generate one still plus narration per scene.
3. Inspect pacing and narration, use imported clips when needed, and render/check a short MP4.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

- **Hardware:** Author lists 5 GB for engines/models plus about 5 GB source-build space; recommends 16 GB RAM. Only M4 Pro/24 GB is tested. The stated 8 GB minimum and 16 GB expectations are estimates, not validated machines.
- **Software:** Node 22.13+, pnpm 11, stable Rust/Tauri prerequisites and Xcode Command Line Tools; Chrome for Remotion. FFmpeg/ffprobe are used for measurements and checking rendered files.
- **Platforms:** Only macOS Apple Silicon has been developed/tested. App/script path requires macOS 13.3+; the pinned picture runtime requires macOS 26. Intel Mac runtimes are unavailable; Windows and Linux are planned, not verified.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers Framebop code. Required Remotion rendering has its own non-open-source license; company use and redistribution need separate review. Models/voices retain individual terms shown before download; optional models can restrict commercial use.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers Framebop code. Required Remotion rendering has its own non-open-source license; company use and redistribution need separate review. Models/voices retain individual terms shown before download; optional models can restrict commercial use.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

### Limitations

Current alpha: packaged rendering is incomplete, AI video decoding is too slow, and vector/sprite animation is a prototype with test art. Licensing documentation lists unresolved packaging/notices questions. Do not describe the complete pipeline as entirely open source. [Source 1](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eaabak/framebop-studio/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

## earshot

Audio, music & voice · AI Foley and sound effects · AI agents for code-authored media production

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-17; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a contextual primary evaluation support the narrow sound-matching use. Independent reproduction, setup, maintenance and full dependency verification remain incomplete, so quality remains unverified.

Scope: CPU matching and ranking of isolated sound effects through the documented CLAP/AST route. Excludes aesthetic judgment, busy-mixture labels, forge realism and hosted Gemini critique.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · verified:** Reviewed the primary evaluation’s held-out 125-file sample, filename-based labels, 3% pair base rate, broad versus exact-variant precision, recall and uncertainty from only three wrong strong pairs. It supports a bounded isolated-effect matching use: reported strong-match precision is 98% broad class and 61% exact variant. This does not establish taste, other sound domains or forge realism, and was not rerun by AI Media Scout. [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Create a Python environment, install CPU PyTorch and the source package with desired extras, then use doctor --load. First model use downloads about 1.4 GB. MCP keeps models loaded across calls; ordinary CLI processes pay the cold start each time. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT code. README identifies CLAP weights as Apache-2.0 and AST as BSD-3-Clause; source recordings and reference films retain their own licenses and are not redistributed. Audio-to-code fitting remains derivative of the input recording. Local CPU/storage; optional Gemini API charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Cannot judge taste, rights, speech content or provenance. Event tags are weaker inside mixtures; the scorecard describes seven reference films. Learned critics can be optimized without audible improvement, and tiny author-only listening tests do not prove realism.

### How it uses AI

CLAP text-audio embeddings and an AudioSet tagger give an agent sound descriptions, search/ranking and event evidence. Forge uses those learned critics to search procedural sound recipes; conventional meters and synthesis are separate parts. [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)

### Introduction

CLAP text-audio embeddings and an AudioSet tagger give an agent sound descriptions, search/ranking and event evidence. Forge uses those learned critics to search procedural sound recipes; conventional meters and synthesis are separate parts. [Source 1](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)

### What it is good for

Finding and comparing sound effects, inspecting sound-to-picture timing and iterating agent-authored motion-design soundtracks on a CPU. [Source 1](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)

### Demo & examples

The project links a 22-second launch film and publishes benchmark methods. In one held-out 125-file library, strong matches reached 98% broad-class precision but 61% exact-variant precision at the stated 3% base rate. These are developer evaluations with disclosed tuning/domain limits, not universal audio-quality proof. [Source 1](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)

### Install

Create a Python environment, install CPU PyTorch and the source package with desired extras, then use doctor --load. First model use downloads about 1.4 GB. MCP keeps models loaded across calls; ordinary CLI processes pay the cold start each time. [Source 1](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/VALIDATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create a Python environment, install CPU PyTorch and the source package with desired extras, then use doctor --load. First model use downloads about 1.4 GB. MCP keeps models loaded across calls; ordinary CLI processes pay the cold start each time.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Leonxlnx/earshot
```


```sh
cd earshot
```


```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python -m pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -e ".[mcp,llm,dev]"
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python -m earshot doctor --load
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. ### How it uses AI

A parametric CAD application exposes a built-in MCP server so an external AI agent can construct, inspect, measure and revise editable solid models. The geometry kernel itself is conventional CAD, not a neural generator. [Source](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A parametric CAD application exposes a built-in MCP server so an external AI agent can construct, inspect, measure and revise editable solid models. The geometry kernel itself is conventional CAD, not a neural generator. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Agent-assisted simple-part modeling with editable parameters, a feature timeline and STEP/STL/3MF export for further review. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows a Raspberry Pi case and links an external rover made through the MCP server. Its 75-of-76 reference-part checks are limited geometry comparisons, not proof of universal CAD equivalence or physically printable output. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install an official desktop release and ensure solidcraft-cli is available, or build the documented Rust source. Connect a compatible external AI agent to the MCP server; use a small headless design before enabling the optional live-app bridge. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install an official desktop release and ensure solidcraft-cli is available, or build the documented Rust source. Connect a compatible external AI agent to the MCP server; use a small headless design before enabling the optional live-app bridge.

```sh
cargo run --release -p solidcraft -- --sample
```


```sh
claude mcp add solidcraft -- solidcraft-cli mcp
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

1. Ask the connected agent to create a simple bracket with named dimensions.
2. Inspect and measure the geometry, change one parameter and review the rebuilt feature timeline.
3. Export an editable CAD file, then independently inspect dimensions, topology and manufacturing suitability.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Rust/cargo for source builds; desktop release plus solidcraft-cli for MCP. An external model/agent client is required for AI authoring. The README does not state a universal minimum Rust or OS version.
- **Platforms:** Documented downloads include Windows x64/ARM64/x86, macOS Apple Silicon/Intel and Linux x86_64/ARM64. The browser CAD app is available, but desktop MCP setup should not be inferred for the browser version.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE) [Source 2](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed; the README also offers an MIT alternative. Third-party code/assets and the external agent/model keep separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed; the README also offers an MIT alternative. Third-party code/assets and the external agent/model keep separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external agent/model service.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The project calls itself early but usable for simple parts, with known geometry gaps. Render lighting is a preview; HDRI, refraction and texture maps are not implemented. Unsigned installers, reference-part tests and an editable rover are not complete platform or fabrication qualification. [Source 1](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/tangency-labs/solidcraft/blob/main/README.md)

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **Diffusion Illusions** · needs-license-review: The project has published demonstrations, but a complete software license was not retrieved from this repository. Public source and a paper do not establish open-source permission. [Source](https://github.com/RyannDaGreat/Diffusion-Illusions)
- **Deep Inverse Halftoning** · needs-license-review: The complete grant is custom, requires written notice for commercial use and includes publication-citation conditions. It was not classified as a verified standard open-source grant. [Source](https://github.com/MenghanXia/InverseHalftoning/blob/master/LICENSE.txt)
- **Predictive Reversible Halftoning** · needs-license-review: The complete MIT-like grant adds a mandatory paper citation in copies/substantial works. The custom grant is not silently relabeled MIT. [Source](https://github.com/nicholaslck/PredictiveReversibleHalftoning/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **GitLab AI Music Generator** · needs-license-review: The current root tree and LICENSE lookup did not establish a complete software grant for its Music Transformer MIDI workflow. [Source](https://gitlab.com/collective.ai/ai-music-generator)
- **illusionFrame** · excluded: The reviewed CLI defines a subcommand parser but no implemented subcommands, and the tree did not establish a working generation path. A roadmap/scaffold is not a usable creative AI tool. [Source](https://github.com/juan-garassino/illusionFrame/blob/main/src/illusionframe/cli.py)
- **Neural Texture Engine** · excluded: The model loader labels default weights as development initialization to be replaced by trained weights; the implementation creates hand-set kernels. A usable pretrained creative upscaling route was not established. [Source](https://github.com/nff747/neural-texture-engine/blob/main/src/ai/QuantizedModel.ts)
- **ARTTOO** · excluded: The capability catalogue explicitly describes proposed, unimplemented contracts; the current state lists drawing/editor foundations. A working AI tattoo-design workflow was not established in this source pass. [Source](https://github.com/zhenrez/ARTTOO/blob/main/docs/authority/product/CAPABILITY_CATALOGUE.md)
- **PlantGen** · excluded: The README explicitly says the deterministic plant generator does not use AI. L-systems and procedural meshes alone do not meet the requested AI-only scope. [Source](https://github.com/jferments/plantgen)
- **Earthloom** · excluded: The reviewed project maps scientific data into deterministic artwork without an established learned or agent-authoring workflow. [Source](https://github.com/wzbuilds/earthloom)
- **Smalti** · excluded: Conventional mosaic optimization and optional PyTorch acceleration do not by themselves establish an AI contribution. [Source](https://github.com/timfennell/smalti)
- **lysy01528-hash/dkyj-director** · excluded: The rewritten download-oriented README conflicts with the identified upstream project’s actual Blender/source-package requirements. Provenance and an implemented creative AI path were not established; its download instructions were not followed. [Source](https://github.com/lysy01528-hash/dkyj-director)
- **FORMA** · excluded: The current tree contains design documents and license files, with no Rust/MCP implementation. Its proposed font-editing interface is not yet an available creative AI tool. [Source](https://github.com/luisfarfan/forma)
- **Influencer Studio** · excluded: The current tree contains README, model/license metadata and an empty workflows placeholder; a working ComfyUI studio implementation was not established. [Source](https://github.com/OpenInfluencerStudio/influencer-studio)
- **cporter202/open-creator** · excluded: The attribution file explicitly identifies this as a mirror of krillinai/OpenCreator with an added promotional block. It is not counted as a distinct tool. [Source](https://github.com/cporter202/open-creator/blob/main/ATTRIBUTION.md)
- **VRM Voice Avatar** · excluded: The reviewed repository implements procedural poses, audio-driven mouth movement and a generic file bus. A named model or concrete working AI voice integration was not established in this pass; an agent setup prompt alone does not make the viewer an AI tool.
